# T10 — GUEST & STAFF-PANEL API ROUTES · phases P04501–P05000

> ## 🔁 SWEEP #8 — this file's 25 route files are no longer one terminal's
>
> Sweep #8 re-cut the territories from the real file structure. **`app/api/guest/**` moved to
> terminal 3** (the basket and placing an order), which re-executed **P04502–P04540** — all 39 ✅ —
> and re-asserted the same ground with 80 fresh rows (`P56981`–`P57060`, in `T3.md`). Another
> sweep-#8 terminal re-ran the `menu-data`, `health` and `rt-config` rows in the same window; both
> sets of marks are here, and **neither terminal touched the other's rows** — two terminals editing
> one row is how three ledger collisions have already happened.
>
> **Two of T3's 39 were passing on a false premise, and both are now annotated rather than quietly
> re-ticked.** `P04509` and `P04527` asserted that the floor snapshot is dropped "using the
> restaurant the RPC itself resolved" — the preference order really was in the code, but **neither
> RPC has ever returned that field**, so the branch was dead and T9's finding F20 was never closed
> for the body it was written about. `P04530` is narrowed the same way: a bell tap that creates no
> row no longer throws away the whole floor's shared read. Both are fixed in sweep #8's items 4
> and 5; the rows keep their ids and carry the correction in their notes.

Territory (write access): `app/api/guest/**` · `app/api/r/**` · `app/api/editor/**` ·
`app/api/kitchen/**` · `app/api/tablet/**` · `app/api/inventory/**` · `app/api/aggregators/**` ·
`app/api/blocked/**` · `app/api/health/**` · `app/api/log/**` · `app/api/maintenance/**` ·
`app/api/issue-media/**` · `app/api/rt-config/**` · `app/api/panel-login/**` ·
`app/api/panel-logout/**` · `app/api/panel-profile/**` · `app/api/staff-login/**` ·
`app/api/staff-logout/**` · `app/login/**` · `app/staff-login/**`

**Budget, as PLANNED** — 40% correctness reads · 20% project-rule conformance · 15% watched running
on port 4110 · 10% screenshots actually read (desktop 1280×800 + Samsung A35 360×780 dpr3) ·
10% traced across panels · 5% judgment about a real restaurant.

**Budget, as ACTUALLY SPENT — stated honestly, because it is not the same shape.** This territory is
25 server-side route files and two rendered pages: 10,733 lines, of which ~10,400 have no pixels at
all. So the phases came out heavier on reading than the plan allowed and lighter on screenshots,
because that is where the answers were. What the evidence actually consists of:

* **~300 correctness reads** — every line of all 25 files, not a sample. Three of the four faults
  were found this way and could not have been found any other way (they only appear when the
  database is failing).
* **~95 conformance rows** — the per-route gate, tenant scoping, egress discipline, idempotency,
  the clash gates, the money caps, the compliance rules, and the things the owner has already
  decided (which are marked as NOT findings).
* **74 watched running** on port 4110 — a scripted pass over the live routes signed out, as a
  manager, a kitchen user, a waiter and the admin. Second pass: **74 checks, 0 failed, 0 extra
  logins** (the shared session cache held, so the owner's phone was never pinged). On top of that
  **89 more** from `verify:tablet --base http://127.0.0.1:4110 --read-only` and **49** from the new
  `verify:panel-api`.
* **4 screenshots read, not just taken** — `/login` and `/staff-login` at both sizes, with the
  rendered character count, the submit button's measured box, and the position of the invisible
  bot field asserted at each.
* **~20 traced across panels** — issue-media → `lib/issues` → the signed link the manager and owner
  screens read · the guest outbox → both guest routes · `realtime.js` → rt-config · the inventory
  purchase form → the inventory route · BlockedView → `/api/blocked` · the kitchen/tablet/manager
  twins of eight shared endpoints.
* **~12 judgment calls** — recorded as the two 🟢 built and the four 🟡 listed, plus the places I
  decided something was deliberate rather than broken.

Every file in the territory has at least one phase; the four `[...path]` catch-alls, the three
guest routes, the two login pages and the four login/logout/profile routes have ten or more.

## THIRD PASS — 2026-08-18, after the owner's follow-up work

All 500 re-run from the top a THIRD time, after building #7 (a saved guest order that names no
restaurant is refused instead of being filed under restaurant #1), #8 (the purchase form asks before
a duplicate line) and #9 (a blocked device goes completely black), and after rebasing onto a `main`
that had moved 99 commits.

* **87 live checks against the running app on port 4110 — 0 failed**, signed out and as manager /
  kitchen / waiter / admin, including the three new behaviours driven end to end. **3 real sign-ins**
  for the whole run (the shared session cache held), so no rate limit was approached and the owner's
  phone was never pinged.
* **28 static gates — all green**, including `verify:read-guards` and `verify:write-paths`, the two
  that were red on the first pass.
* **`verify:panel-api` is now 72 checks.** Every rule was proved to FAIL when its fix is reverted.

**One real problem the re-run caught, which nothing else would have.** `verify:panel-cache` went red:
the three panel files changed for #8 and #9 (`editor/inventory.js`, `kitchen/app.js`,
`tablet/app.js`) still carried their OLD `?v=` content hash in the panel HTML. `vercel.json` allows a
stale panel asset for up to 24h, so staff would have kept running the previous file and neither the
blocked wall nor the duplicate question would have appeared for them — the exact "a device ran a
weeks-old panel" trap. Re-stamped; all 57 panel assets now carry their own hash. **This is the reason
to re-run the whole thing rather than only the parts that were touched.**

Result key: ✅ pass · ❌ real problem (row says where it is written up) · ⏭ skipped, with a reason.

## FOURTH PASS — sweep #7, 2026-08-22 · **all 500 re-run · 0 regressions** · 500 new checks added

Re-run against `origin/main` **b64951ad**, in worktree `../wt-s7-t10`, on port **4210** (proved mine
before anything was believed: `lsof` says the listener's cwd is the worktree).

**Every one of the 500 rows below was re-executed, top to bottom — not a sample, and not only the
red ones. 500 passed. Nothing that was green in sweep #6 has gone red.** That is the answer this
ledger exists to give, and this is the first run that can give it.

The re-run is MECHANICAL and reproducible: one assertion per id, read from the source as it stands
today, so each `result` below is an observation rather than a memory. Its own first draft was wrong
in a way worth recording — it stripped block comments BEFORE line comments, so the `/*` inside
`// … under /api/kitchen/*), shapes …` opened a fake block that swallowed 30 KB of the kitchen route
and failed **28 rows that were all fine**. Strip line comments FIRST. Four more rows then failed on
the harness's own sloppiness (an `indexOf("logAction")` that found the import, a REJECTED marker
looked for in the wrong file, a constant renamed to `STALE_CLAIM_MS`, and a match window too small).
**Every single one of the 32 was the detector being wrong, not the product** — the lesson the INDEX
already records, learned again.

**Two rows had their EXPECTATION move, and both moved because the product legitimately changed** —
improvement #7 was built on 2026-08-18 ("I agree to 7"). The ids are unchanged, as they must be:

* **P04508** — was *"an absent `restaurantId` still falls back to restaurant #1 (legacy shape)"*.
  Now: **absent OR malformed is REFUSED** with `unknown_restaurant`. Driven live.
* **P04528** — the same move on `/api/guest/call-waiter`.

**Three real problems were found this run, all in NEW ground, all fixed in this branch** — see
`.claude/sweep/T10-findings.md`. Each left a guard behind, and each guard was proven to go red when
its fix is reverted.

Result key: ✅ pass · ❌ real problem (row says where it is written up) · ⏭ skipped, with a reason.

**These are SECOND-PASS results.** All 500 were re-run from the top after the fixes landed. On the
first pass four rows were ❌ (P04566, P04573, P04687 and the F4 row inside the inventory block);
each is now ✅ and its note names the fix. Nothing is marked green that was not re-checked.

## SWEEP #7 · THE 500 NEW CHECKS — `P19601`–`P20100`

**Nobody handed me a checklist this time.** These were planned after reading all 11,825 lines of the
territory (it was 10,733 in sweep #6 — the product grew by ~1,100 lines), and aimed deliberately at
ground the 500 rows above do **not** cover:

| block | ids | what it asks | rows |
|---|---|---|---|
| A · egress and bounding | P19601–P19662 | every `.select(` in all 25 files: does it name its columns, and is it bounded? | 62 |
| B · tenant scoping | P19663–P19759 | every read and write of a tenant table: does it name the restaurant, or is it keyed by an already-verified id? | 97 |
| C · the print-helper surface | P19760–P19799 | migs 335 / 336 / 338 / 341 / 351 — **added after the sweep-6 ledger was written**, and where all three of this run's faults live | 40 |
| D · the editor's POST / PATCH / DELETE | P19800–P19841 | 3,000 lines the old ledger barely touches | 42 |
| E · the inventory route, end to end | P19842–P19891 | 830 lines with only one old row (F4) | 50 |
| F · twin parity | P19892–P19947 | the same question asked of kitchen / tablet / editor / inventory, so a fix to one is a fix to all | 56 |
| G · judgement | P19948–P19982 | "is this how it *should* work for a real restaurant?" | 35 |
| H · watched running on :4210 | P19983–P20082 | 100 checks driven headless, signed out and as manager / kitchen / waiter / admin | 100 |
| I · the guards that watch this territory | P20083–P20100 | are they green, do they cover the new fixes, and can they fail? | 18 |

**Result: 500 executed, 500 pass, 0 outstanding.** The three faults this run found are already fixed
in the branch, so their rows read ✅ and say `FIXED (item N)`.

### What Blocks A and B actually taught, which is worth more than their 159 greens

Both blocks started by flagging **26 "unscoped" or "unbounded" reads. Every one of them was correct
code and my detector was wrong** — the third sweep running to learn this. The four shapes that fool
a naive walker, written down so the next one does not re-file them:

1. **A chain that spans a blank line.** The first walker stopped at one; half this codebase's chains
   are formatted across several lines. Walk to the terminating `;` at depth 0 instead.
2. **A builder head.** `let q = sb.from("orders").select(…)` with the filters *and the `.limit()`*
   applied to `q` twenty lines later. No single-chain walker can follow it — assert those by name.
3. **A write's returning `.select()`.** `.update({…}).eq(…).select("id")` is bounded by the write's
   own filters; demanding a `.limit()` on it is nonsense.
4. **A read already narrowed to ONE PARENT ROW** — `.eq("order_id", …)`, `.eq("session_id", …)`,
   `.in("id", ids)`. The parent *is* the bound; an order has tens of lines, not millions.

And four genuinely-unscoped-looking chains that are each deliberately so, with the reason in the
code beside them: `panel-profile` writes only the caller's OWN row keyed by the verified `u.id`;
`feedback` does a check-then-403 on `row.restaurant_id !== rid` instead of an `.eq`; the menu-item
create probe **must** be unscoped because `menu_items.id` is the global primary key; and the generic
CRUD upsert sets `body.restaurant_id = rid` two lines above itself.

### The one thing recorded but NOT fixed at the time — ✅ HE PICKED IT (2026-08-28), and it is fixed

**The waiter tablet's two `categories` reads carry no `.limit()`** — the only place in the territory
where CLAUDE.md's *"column list AND `.limit()`"* is not literally satisfied. Left alone on purpose:
the list is scoped, it names its columns, it is about ten rows on a real restaurant, and the guest
menu's own read is shaped the same way. The editor's twin *does* cap it at 500, so the inconsistency
is real and entirely cosmetic. It was raised as an idea rather than committed as a fix, because a
`.limit()` on a ten-row list is a commit that changes nothing and a line the next reader has to
wonder about — so it was his call rather than my tidiness. Row **P19636** (not P19652–P19654, as
this paragraph first said — the block-A ids shifted while the harness was being corrected, and the
citation was never re-derived. Fixed here by counting, not by remembering).

**He picked it on 2026-08-28** — *"yes do 3rd one"*. Both reads now carry `.limit(500)`, matching
the manager panel's twin. Re-derived after the change: **63 reads on the tablet route, all bounded,
no exception carried**, so this territory now satisfies CLAUDE.md's *"column list AND `.limit()`"*
with nothing left over. `verify:panel-api` asks about both category reads by name, so the ceiling
cannot quietly come off again (proven: removing one turns it red).

# SWEEP #8 · TERMINAL 10 — THE WAITER TABLET · phases P63701–P64232

> **This block is the WAITER TABLET, not this file's original territory.** Sweep #8 re-cut the
> territories from the real file structure and terminal 10 became
> `app/tablet/**` · `app/api/tablet/**` · `public/panels/tablet/*` (app.js, style.css, index.html).
> The rows above about `app/api/guest/**` and the login pages belong to other terminals now — this
> block was APPENDED, nothing above it was rewritten, and the 24 rows above whose subject is a file
> this terminal owns were re-run in place and carry an `s8·T10 2026-09-03` note.

**532 written, 532 executed, 532 ✅.** Of the pre-allocated block `P63701`–`P64700`, ids
`P64233`–`P64700` are unused.

**Planned by MEASURING, not by having ideas.** 546 named things were enumerated from the four files
(44 POST branches + 9 GET endpoints, 177 panel functions, 25 route helpers, 293 style rules) and
crossed against every existing ledger row and every guard that touches this panel: **171 of them
were named by nothing at all.** Those, plus a full grid of endpoint × property, are what this block
aims at. The endpoint grid is generated FROM the dispatcher's own conditions rather than typed, so a
branch added next year is asked the same questions without anyone remembering this block exists.

**Also re-run in this pass, in the files where they live:** 177 earlier rows across 15 ledgers whose
subject is a file this terminal owns — **176 ✅ · 1 REGRESSION (P12639, found and fixed as item 1)**.
One row was deliberately left untouched: `P37391` (`verify:avlive-release`), whose subject is a guard
that reads the client stack this terminal may not go near.

**Seven of my own detectors were wrong before any of them was believed**, and every one is written
into the check that replaced it: a colour parser that could not read Chrome's `color(srgb …)`
notation and so called a plainly readable tile number 1.19:1; a sabotage that left its needle as a
SUBSTRING (`/cf-nudge/` still matches "cf-nudgeZZ"); a scan that matched an OBITUARY comment and
reported the bug it describes as live; a dead-CSS check that did not know a class can be BUILT
(`t-${st.cls}`) and named ten live ones; a tap-size check that counted an `aria-hidden` badge as a
control; a realtime check that looked for `subscribe(topic)` when this panel hands `LFH_RT.start` a
handler MAP; and a dev server rebuilding underneath the run, whose `/_next/` 403s are not a product
fault. Sweeps keep re-learning that a red row is the detector more often than the product.

# SWEEP #8 · TERMINAL 10 · ROUND 2 — the waiter tablet, planned again · phases P64233–P64588 + P64589–P64700 + P99951–P99982

> **The owner's word after round 1 was merged and deployed:** *"after making it live and merging plan
> 500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everthing within your boundries and test
> everything again if any error left"*.

**500 written, 500 executed, 500 ✅.** **THE BLOCK IS NOW EXACTLY SPENT** — `P63701`–`P64700` is
full, and the 32 ids `P99951`–`P99982` were claimed from the registry for the shortfall (the claim
was pushed to `main` on its own before a single row was written). ⚠️ **After that claim the
five-digit space has SEVENTEEN ids left**; `INDEX.md` now carries the warning and what to do about
it, because `verify:ledger-index` matches `^| P[0-9]{5}` and would neither count nor protect a
six-digit id.

**PLANNED BY MEASURING, NOT BY HAVING IDEAS.** 775 named things were enumerated from the territory
as it stands *after* round 1 and items 11–15 — 53 endpoints, 180 panel functions, 28 route helpers,
the style rules, the HTML ids, the `data-` attributes, the state keys, the RPCs, the tables and every
lib import — and crossed against every existing ledger row and every guard that touches this panel.
**196 were named by nothing at all.** This round is aimed at those, at the five things the owner had
just picked, and at the two surfaces round 1 barely reached: the ORDER-TAKING screen and the
PARAMETER SPACE of the GET endpoints.

**What it found:** three dead controls in the panel, all the same shape — something left behind when
the thing that read it was replaced. An abandoned `data-qty` on the quantity steppers (the fix for
the stale-DOM read in July stopped reading it and its own comment says so); a third copy of the
whole-order allergy fact (`data-orderwide`, which nothing reads and nothing styles); and **a whole
collapsed step of the payment sheet** — `.pay-other-pick` and its one button — rendered into the DOM
on every settle, permanently hidden, with an attribute nothing read. Removing the step then left a
live line reaching for it, which would have thrown on the first tap of "Other"; found by driving it.
Fixed as item 16.

**Seven of my own detectors were wrong in this round too**, and every one is written into the check
that replaced it: a slice that ran past its function into a NEIGHBOUR's code (twice — it read
`reasonPrompt`'s "A reason is required." as the blocked-device wall's wording); a case-sensitive test
for `floorDelegationBound`; a nested function `fn()` could not find; a scan that asserted a mechanism
the function does not use; a script-order test that read index.html's explanatory COMMENTS instead of
its `<script>` tags; a `data-` reader test that did not know `[data-quick='accept']` or a stylesheet
counts as a reader; and three expectations about which of two CORRECT gates answers first.

**Also recorded, measured and deliberately NOT filed:** three classes written as hooks and used as
neither (`accept-all`, `sb-nlbl`, `opt-alg`) — each sits on an element its siblings already style,
so removing them changes nothing on screen; the check now fails only if a FOURTH appears.
